19:T1fe3,## Why

\`docker_nodes.status\` is currently driven by a separate \`container-control-plane\` Node service that the CF Worker cron \`agent-hot-pool\` forwards to every 5 minutes. In prod today that service runs on a host we no longer reliably track, and (verified 2026-05-17) it can't SSH the cores — its \`CONTAINERS_SSH_KEY\` is empty.

Result: all six \`milady-core-*\` nodes have been stuck in \`offline\` for days even though SSH + \`docker info\` succeed instantly from the orchestrator (provisioning-worker daemon) host using the key we set up there last night for provision/stop.

Concretely from the orchestrator VM:

\`\`\`
$ for ip in 88.99.66.168 178.63.251.122 138.201.80.125 \\
            85.10.193.52  136.243.47.243 195.201.57.227; do
    ssh -i ~/.ssh/clawdnet_nodes root@\$ip \\
      "docker info --format '{{.ID}}'"
  done
3378395a-...
5e0b07c7-...
aab68679-...
ed32c4bb-...
13241c37-...
d51175ba-...
\`\`\`

All six respond. Yet \`SELECT node_id, status FROM docker_nodes\` returns \`offline\` for all of them. The check itself is fine; the host that runs it is the wrong one.

## What this PR does

Folds the loop into the daemon that already has everything it needs:

- the \`CONTAINERS_SSH_KEY\` load-bearing for provision and stop (PR #7746),
- direct Neon write access via \`DATABASE_URL\`,
- a 30s poll cycle that's a perfect carrier for a 5-min cadence.

\`pollCycle\` now calls \`dockerNodeManager.healthCheckAll()\` gated by \`lastNodeHealthCheckAt + nodeHealthIntervalMs <= now\`, and logs a summary line per tick (\`total/healthy/unhealthy\`) so ops can spot regressions without grepping CF tail.

The legacy CF Worker cron route is left in place — it returns 503 when no \`CONTAINER_CONTROL_PLANE_URL\` is configured, which is harmless. A follow-up can remove the forward + the unused \`container-control-plane\` package once the daemon has been proven in prod for a week.

## Env knobs

- \`WORKER_NODE_HEALTH_INTERVAL\` (ms) — defaults to **5 minutes**, mirroring the legacy CRON_FANOUT cadence for \`agent-hot-pool\`. Set to a smaller value during the initial rollout if you want faster recovery, or a larger one once steady-state.

## Files touched

- \`packages/scripts/cloud/admin/daemons/provisioning-worker.ts\` — adds \`dockerNodeManager\` to \`loadDeps\`, a \`processNodeHealthCheckCycle\` helper, and the gated invocation in \`pollCycle\`. ~75 lines net.

## Behavior under load

\`dockerNodeManager.healthCheckAll()\` already iterates with \`Promise.allSettled\` and the per-node \`healthCheckNode\` has its own \`MAX_RETRIES * SSH_TIMEOUT\` budget (~40s worst case for a fully offline node). So even with all six cores unreachable the worst-case node health tick stays well under one minute and the heartbeat / job pickup cycles aren't starved.

## Test plan

- [x] Daemon parses + cloud-shared typecheck clean on touched paths
- [ ] Roll to staging orchestrator → tail journal, verify a \`node health check cycle complete\` log fires once at boot and then every 5 minutes
- [ ] After 5–10 min, query Neon: every reachable milady-core flips to \`healthy\` with a fresh \`last_health_check\` (this is the load-bearing observation)
- [ ] Provision a fresh agent on staging — \`getAvailableNode\` should now pick a milady-core (since they're healthy with 50 capacity each)
- [ ] Roll to prod orchestrator (\`systemctl restart eliza-provisioning-worker\`) and repeat the observation

## Rollback

Single revert. The legacy CF Worker cron path is unchanged and still gets ticked every 5 min — it just no-ops when the control-plane URL is unset. Reverting this PR puts us back to where we are today (daemon doesn't health-check, control-plane is missing) without any data migration.

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR moves Docker node health-checking from a broken `container-control-plane` service (whose SSH key is empty) into the provisioning-worker daemon that already holds a working `CONTAINERS_SSH_KEY`. The `agentHotPoolResponse` in `container-control-plane` no longer calls `healthCheckAll()`, preventing a write race between the two processes; the legacy CF Worker cron still fires but returns 503 from the CF layer when `CONTAINER_CONTROL_PLANE_URL` is unset.

- **provisioning-worker.ts**: Adds `dockerNodeManager` to the lazy-loaded dep singleton, implements `processNodeHealthCheckCycle` (iterates `healthCheckAll` results into a `{ total, healthy, unhealthy }` summary), and gates invocation in `pollCycle` with `lastNodeHealthCheckAt` — initialized to 0 so the first tick always runs a check. A new `WORKER_NODE_HEALTH_INTERVAL` env knob (default 5 min) controls cadence.
- **container-control-plane/src/index.ts**: Removes the `healthCheckAll()` call and the `healthChecks` field from the `agentHotPoolResponse` JSON, shrinking the response payload but not breaking the hot-pool pre-pull or capacity-report paths.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

Safe to merge — the change is a targeted relocation of an existing operation to a host that already has the required SSH credentials, with no data migration and a clean rollback path.

The provisioning cycle, heartbeat cycle, and node health check each run in independent try/catch blocks, so a failure in one cannot stall the others. The `lastNodeHealthCheckAt = 0` initialization guarantees a fresh status snapshot at startup. Removing `healthCheckAll()` from the CF cron handler eliminates the write race without affecting the pre-pull or capacity-report paths. No callers import the now-unexported `processProvisioningWorkerCycle`/`processHeartbeatCycle` functions. The legacy CF cron path is unchanged and harmlessly no-ops when the control-plane URL is unset.

1b:T19b0,## Root cause

Fresh sandboxes provisioned with `:develop` consistently failed health check at 180s. The container started, Node ran, but never reached the HTTP listen.

Container logs:
```
[wallet][os-store] boot hydrate skipped: Cannot find package 'jose' imported from /app/plugins/plugin-elizacloud/dist/node/index.node.js
[eliza-autonomous] Failed to start: Error [ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND]: Cannot find package 'jose' ...
```

Inspecting the image:

```
/app/node_modules/jose -> /app/packages/app-core/node_modules/jose
/app/packages/app-core/node_modules/jose -> ../../../node_modules/jose
```

Two-hop circular symlink. Node's ESM resolver can't traverse it → import fails → process never finishes boot → container hangs in `ep_poll` until daemon kills it.

## How the loop was formed

`link-docker-local-app-packages.mjs` links app-core's owned root deps (`jose`, `@node-rs/argon2`) in two passes:
1. `packages/app-core/node_modules/<dep>` (for app-core's local resolution)
2. `/app/node_modules/<dep>` (for transitive imports from other plugins)

Each pass calls `resolveDependencyPackageDir` with `[packageDir, repoRoot]`. After the first pass linked `app-core/node_modules/jose → /app/node_modules/jose`, the second pass's resolver searched packageDir first, saw the freshly-created symlink's target had `package.json` (`fs.existsSync` follows symlinks), and returned the symlink path verbatim.

`linkDependencyPackage`'s equality check `path.resolve(packageDir) === path.resolve(target)` compared the symlink path against the target — different strings, so the function proceeded:
1. `removePath(target)` — **deleted the real npm-installed jose at `/app/node_modules/jose`**
2. symlinked target → packageDir (the now-broken intermediate symlink)

End state: circular symlink. Real npm install gone.

## Fix

`resolveDependencyPackageDir` now returns `fs.realpathSync(dir)` instead of the raw join path. The realpath collapses any prior symlink, so when the second-pass call resolves jose it returns `/app/node_modules/jose` (the actual install). `linkDependencyPackage`'s equality check then correctly detects `target === packageDir` and skips — preserving the real npm install at root.

## How this was missed

The first `:develop` builds without Shaw's `baseDirs` patch resolved only at root, so this couldn't happen. The bug was introduced in `06dc596` ("fix(docker): resolve app-core owned dependencies") which added `baseDirs: [packageDir, repoRoot]` so the resolver searches both. Once that landed, every subsequent `:develop` build had the circular symlink baked in.

## Test plan

- [ ] CI build-agent-image.yml succeeds on this branch
- [ ] In the resulting image, `ls -la /app/node_modules/jose` is a real directory (not a symlink)
- [ ] In the resulting image, `ls -la /app/packages/app-core/node_modules/jose` is a symlink → `/app/node_modules/jose` (one hop, not circular)
- [ ] Manual provision of a fresh sandbox via the dashboard transitions from `pending` → `running` (not stuck in health timeout)
- [ ] Existing PR #7780's consolidation + tsx fix still works (this branch contains all those commits)

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR fixes a circular symlink bug in the Docker image build process where `resolveDependencyPackageDir` was returning raw (potentially symlinked) paths, causing `linkDependencyPackage`'s equality guard to fail and subsequently overwrite the real npm-installed package with a circular symlink.

- **Core fix**: `realIfExists` now calls `fs.realpathSync(dir)` before returning, so the equality check `path.resolve(packageDir) === path.resolve(target)` correctly identifies when source and destination resolve to the same on-disk location and skips the symlink creation.
- **Bun store search path** gets the same `realpathSync` treatment, keeping consistency across both resolution strategies.
- The README update reflects the rename of the Docker workflow file from `image.yaml` to `build-agent-image.yml`.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

Safe to merge — the change is narrowly scoped to the symlink resolution helper and directly fixes the broken two-hop symlink that was causing containers to hang at boot.

The fix correctly collapses symlinks via `realpathSync` before the equality guard in `linkDependencyPackage` runs, preventing the second-pass from treating the newly-created `app-core/node_modules/jose` symlink as a distinct source and overwriting the real root install. Both the first-pass and bun-store search paths receive the same treatment.

The `realIfExists` catch fallback in `link-docker-local-app-packages.mjs` is worth tracking but was flagged in a prior review thread.

The catch fallback returning the raw `dir` rather than `null` is a residual risk (already flagged in prior review thread). |

    Note over S: Result no circular symlink real install preserved

<sub>Reviews (2): Last reviewed commit: ["chore(clean): trim verbose comment + upd..."](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/commit/e3f61404f7adadd6e84ac3a5abb852f85ccaa909) | [Re-trigger Greptile](https://app.greptile.com/api/retrigger?id=32536457)</sub>

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1c:T184c,## Problem

`image.yaml` (release-triggered, manual dispatch) was failing every run on develop with:

```
Error: Missing package manifest:
  packages/app-core/node_modules/@node-rs/argon2/package.json
  or node_modules/@node-rs/argon2/package.json
    at resolveDependencyPackageDir (link-docker-local-app-packages.mjs:213)
```

Root cause: `Dockerfile.ci` is a pruner-style image that expects pre-built artifacts (host-side `bun install` + per-package dist/) already in the build context. `build-agent-image.yml` (continuous publication of `:develop` / `:stable` / `:latest`) runs that entire pre-build prelude. `image.yaml` never did, and the repo root `.dockerignore` excludes `**/node_modules` on top of that — so the first RUN inside the pruner stage fails on the bun store lookup before any dep can be resolved.

## Approach

Don't duplicate the ~120 lines of pre-build YAML — fold the release trigger into `build-agent-image.yml` and delete `image.yaml`. The Dockerfile, dockerignore, and artifact set are identical; only the trigger surface and tag rules differ.

## Changes

`build-agent-image.yml`:
- Renamed to "Build Agent Image" (now covers all three trigger surfaces).
- Added `release: created` trigger.
- Added `type=ref,event=tag` metadata rule → release tags map verbatim (e.g. `v2.0.0-alpha.176`).
- Folded in the "make package public" curl step from image.yaml.

`image.yaml`: deleted.

## Tag matrix after consolidation

| Trigger | Tags produced |
|---|---|
| push to develop | `:develop`, `:sha-<short>` |
| push to main | `:stable`, `:latest`, `:sha-<short>` |
| release created | `<git-tag>`, `:sha-<short>` |
| workflow_dispatch | `:sha-<short>` (manual reruns) |

## Test plan

- [ ] workflow_dispatch on this branch succeeds (run 25994404893 in progress)
- [ ] Verify GHCR gets a `:sha-<short>` tag from the dispatch run
- [ ] After merge to develop, verify next push produces a fresh `:develop` tag
- [ ] Optional verification: cut a draft release and confirm release-tag publication still works (not blocking merge — fold into next actual release)

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR consolidates the release-triggered `image.yaml` into `build-agent-image.yml` by adding a `release: created` trigger and a `type=ref,event=tag` metadata rule, eliminating ~120 lines of duplicated pre-build YAML. It also fixes a Dockerfile.ci runtime failure where `tsx` was not reliably resolvable from `/app/node_modules` by installing it in a dedicated build stage at `/opt/tsx` and switching `APP_CMD_START` to reference the loader by absolute path.

- **Workflow consolidation**: `release: created` and the "make Docker image public" curl step are folded from the now-deleted `image.yaml` into `build-agent-image.yml`; tag production for push/release/dispatch is governed by a single `tags:` block.
- **Dockerfile.ci tsx stage**: A new `FROM node:${NODE_VERSION}-slim AS tsx` stage installs `tsx@4.21.0` at `/opt/tsx`; the final stage COPYs it in and both `APP_CMD_START` and the `CMD` fallback reference the loader at the full absolute path `/opt/tsx/node_modules/tsx/dist/loader.mjs`.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

Safe to merge for the consolidation and Dockerfile fix; the "make image public" curl call is a silent no-op on every run due to a pre-existing bug in the endpoint URL that was carried over from image.yaml.

The workflow consolidation and tsx stage are structurally sound. However, the "Make Docker image public" step uses `/user/packages/container/elizaos/eliza/visibility` — a user-scoped endpoint with a slash-embedded package name — for an org-owned repository. curl exits 0 on HTTP errors by default, so the step always silently produces a 4xx and the image is never actually made public through this path. This bug was present in the original image.yaml but never reached in practice due to pre-build failures; now that those failures are fixed, the step runs on every successful push and release.

.github/workflows/build-agent-image.yml — specifically the "Make Docker image public" step at the end of the job.

<sub>Reviews (3): Last reviewed commit: ["fix(docker): bake standalone tsx loader ..."](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/commit/63285a4bfc847fa99810ff2f24d8d68ea3526ca7) | [Re-trigger Greptile](https://app.greptile.com/api/retrigger?id=32509810)</sub>

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1d:T67b,## Summary

Hardens the browser fallback stubs used when renderer/browser bundles statically reach server-only modules.

The prior fallback proxy returned the same noop for all property reads but did not behave like a normal function/object under introspection. Browser bundles can hit `prototype`, `ownKeys`, property descriptors, destructuring, or class-ish access while walking imported server-only modules, and the fallback should remain inert instead of throwing.

## What Changed

- Add a shared proxy handler shape to the app-core browser stubs.
- Return the real function `prototype` when code asks for `prototype`.
- Preserve `ownKeys`/`getOwnPropertyDescriptor` behavior so object introspection does not fail.
- Keep calls inert with `apply: () => undefined`.

## Validation

```sh
git diff --check
bun build packages/app-core/src/platform/elizaos-agent-browser-stub.ts \
  packages/app-core/src/platform/elizaos-plugin-elizacloud-browser-stub.ts \
  packages/app-core/src/platform/empty-node-module.ts \
  --target=browser --outdir=/tmp/eliza-stub-bundle-check
```

Result: all three stub entrypoints bundled for browser successfully.

This was also exercised indirectly by the Pixel stock Android APK validation in milady-ai/milady#2153, where keeping server-only agent/app-core code out of the WebView was required for the renderer to mount cleanly.

## Notes

`packages/app-core` typecheck is not included as validation here because a clean sibling worktree without a full local workspace install fails on missing repo dependency declarations before reaching this patch. This PR is intentionally limited to self-contained browser stub behavior.
1e:T1829,## Why

Manually triggering the **Create and publish a Docker image** workflow (\`image.yaml\`) on \`develop\` today fails at the very first RUN inside the Docker build:

\`\`\`
#13 0.536     throw new Error(
#13 0.536 Error: Missing compiled package manifest for @elizaos/agent:
          packages/agent/dist/package.json
#13 ERROR: process "/bin/sh -c node ${APP_CORE_DIR}/scripts/relink-workspace-packages-to-dist.mjs @elizaos/agent"
          did not complete successfully: exit code: 1
\`\`\`

[Run 25990638222](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/25990638222).

\`Dockerfile.ci\` is a *pruner* image — its [first line](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/develop/packages/app-core/deploy/Dockerfile.ci#L3) literally says *"Pre-built artifacts come from GHA (bun install + tsdown + vite build done before docker)"* — and the second RUN attempts \`relink-workspace-packages-to-dist.mjs @elizaos/agent\`, which reads \`packages/agent/dist/package.json\` to wire the symlinks. The image.yaml workflow has always been missing that pre-build; the path simply hasn't been exercised because manual dispatches were rare and the historical release flow shipped a pre-built tree.

Today we needed to re-publish \`:stable\` to propagate recent fixes (DATABASE_URL → POSTGRES_URL bridge in app-core/entry.ts, the new sandbox self-registration, and the agent_delete queue path) to the Hetzner sandboxes. Without an image rebuild those sandboxes stay on the old \`:stable\` from May 13 and crash-loop on PGLite.

## What this PR does

Three steps before the Buildx step:

1. \`oven-sh/setup-bun@v2\` pinned to **1.3.13** (the version this repo standardizes on)
2. \`bun install --frozen-lockfile\` with a workspace-aware install cache
3. \`bun run --cwd packages/agent build:docker-dist\`
   - \`rm -rf dist\`
   - \`tsc --noCheck -p tsconfig.build.json\`
   - \`node ../scripts/prepare-package-dist.mjs packages/agent\` — this is what writes \`packages/agent/dist/package.json\` that the Dockerfile expects

## What this PR deliberately does NOT do

It does **not** replicate the full \`docker-ci-smoke.sh\` pre-build chain (\`build:client\`, \`build:web\`, root \`tsdown\`). Dockerfile.ci's first RUN only needs the agent dist for the workspace-relink step; the web bundle and the rest are produced inside later Docker stages. Keeping the host-side pre-build minimal is intentional — fewer moving pieces, faster build, less surface to drift.

## Verification

- [x] Workflow YAML parses (would-be drift caught by actionlint on CI)
- [ ] Trigger \`gh workflow run image.yaml --ref fix/image-yaml-prebuild\` against this branch — verify the new pre-build step produces \`packages/agent/dist/package.json\` and the subsequent Buildx step proceeds past the relink RUN

## Rollback

Single revert. The added steps are no-op for cached scenarios (Bun cache hit + idempotent build), so reverting puts us back to where we are today (workflow broken on manual dispatch) without any side effects on other jobs.

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds three pre-build steps to `image.yaml` (Bun setup, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, and `bun run --cwd packages/agent build:docker-dist`) so that `packages/agent/dist/package.json` exists in the Docker build context before the `relink-workspace-packages-to-dist.mjs` step runs.

- The root `.dockerignore` was recently updated to explicitly un-ignore `packages/agent/dist/**`, so the host-built artifact does reach the Docker build context.
- However, `Dockerfile.ci` line 28 (`RUN cd \"${AGENT_DIR}\" && npm run build:docker-dist`) starts with `rm -rf dist`, immediately deleting the pre-built directory; it then attempts `tsc`, which is not available because `**/node_modules` is excluded from the Docker context. The build still fails — just one step earlier than before.

<h3>Confidence Score: 2/5</h3>

The workflow change does not fully resolve the Docker build failure because Dockerfile.ci line 28 deletes the pre-built dist and then cannot rebuild it without node_modules.

The host pre-build produces packages/agent/dist/package.json and the .dockerignore correctly preserves it, but Dockerfile.ci line 28 immediately runs rm -rf dist then attempts tsc which is not resolvable because node_modules is excluded from the Docker build context. The Docker build fails at that RUN step and the relink step is never reached.

packages/app-core/deploy/Dockerfile.ci line 28 needs to be removed or guarded so the host-pre-built dist/ is not deleted before the relink step runs.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1f:T8ea,## Summary

Stock Android APK builds can package `libeliza-llama-speculative-shim.so` for diagnostics, but should not automatically enable in-process DFlash solely because the shim is present.

This keeps DFlash auto-enable scoped to branded AOSP builds and leaves stock APKs target-only unless `ELIZA_DFLASH` is explicitly provided. The AOSP path still gets the same behavior as before, and explicit env overrides still work.

## Why

Pixel APK validation showed the current 2B speculative path is functional, but slower than target-only decode for short interactive chat turns:

- `eliza-1-2b` with DFlash: TTFT `11547.4ms`, text total `17124.4ms`, drafted/accepted `8/8`, accept rate `1.0`.
- `eliza-1-2b` target-only: TTFT `4705.3ms`, text total `6175.7ms`.
- Rebuilt stock APK after this gate: env has no `ELIZA_DFLASH`, logcat prints `leaving DFlash opt-in for stock APK`, and `eliza-1-2b` target-only measured TTFT `4689.5ms`, text total `6336.3ms`.
- Final `eliza-1-0_8b` stock APK baseline after the gate: TTFT `2359.5ms`, text total `2359.8ms`; local Kokoro TTS returned `audio/wav`, `100844` bytes, `2.1s` audio in `6040.1ms`.

That means stock Android should not silently choose DFlash until benchmark gating or a faster drafter path lands. Branded AOSP can continue enabling it by default for device-image experiments.

## Validation

- `git diff --check` passed.
- Full local-source Android APK build passed in the integration tree with this Java behavior included.
- Installed rebuilt APK on Pixel 9a (`ai.milady.milady`) and verified:
  - `/api/health` returned `ready:true`, runtime/database ok, `10` plugins loaded, `0` failed.
  - Bun env included `ELIZA_LOCAL_LLAMA=1` and mobile llama caps, but no `ELIZA_DFLASH`.
  - logcat confirmed the stock APK branch: `libeliza-llama-speculative-shim.so present; leaving DFlash opt-in for stock APK`.
  - `eliza-1-0_8b` activation and direct local generation succeeded.

A scoped Gradle Java compile from this clean source-only worktree was blocked before compilation because the checked-out platform project references `packages/native-plugins/gateway/android`, which is not present until the generated/local mobile project alignment step runs. The full APK build path did compile and package the same Java service code.

2a:T1781,## Summary

`Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct Q4_K_M` (~8.5 GB) runs at **1.15 t/s tg** on Intel Arc 140V iGPU via Shaw's Vulkan + W4-B kernels — only ~22% of the LPDDR5X bandwidth ceiling, vs 63-87% on the other Llama / Qwen3-family Q4_K_M models I tested with the **same Vulkan build, same drivers, same `-ngl 99 -c 2048` invocation**.

**n=1 caveat upfront**: I have only tested *Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct Q4_K_M* in the 14B param class on this hardware. The cliff may be Qwen2.5-14B-specific (head-dim / GQA-factor / tile-alignment) rather than a general "14B param-class" issue. A second 14B (Phi-4-14B, Mistral-Nemo-12B) would tell us which. I'm filing it now because the gap is large enough to be worth investigating even at n=1, and the diagnostic ask is the same either way.

## Bandwidth ceiling derivation

For a memory-bandwidth-bound decoder (the steady-state regime on integrated GPUs with Q4_K_M weights), every token needs ~one full pass of the weight bytes. So:

```
ceiling_t_per_s ≈ sustained_bandwidth_GB_per_s / weights_GB
```

For Lunar Lake on-package LPDDR5X-8533:
- Theoretical peak: 8533 MT/s × 16 B/transfer = 136.5 GB/s
- Sustained iGPU-effective (after refresh, CPU-shared, page traffic): **~75 GB/s** (Intel-published ~67 GB/s sustained; rounded up slightly for cool-laptop, fresh-server best case)

So the ceiling-t/s column below is `75 / weights_GB`. The measured t/s is `timings.predicted_per_second` from `/v1/chat/completions`. Efficiency is `measured / ceiling`.

## Bench (cool laptop, no contention, same prompt, fresh server per row)

| Model | Q4_K_M weights | 75 GB/s ceiling | Measured tg | Efficiency |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct | 1.9 GB | 39.5 t/s | 20.0 t/s | **51%** |
| Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct | 4.9 GB | 15.3 t/s | 5.7 t/s | **37%** |
| Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct Q3_K_M | 3.8 GB | 19.7 t/s | 6.3 t/s | 32% |
| **Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct** | **8.5 GB** | **8.8 t/s** | **1.15 t/s** | **13%** |
| Qwen3.6-27B Q4_K_M (body-only, DFlash disabled) | 16 GB | 4.7 t/s | 1.15 t/s | 25% |

(Earlier draft of this issue used a more optimistic 50% sustained-headroom assumption that bumped the column to a "60-87% efficiency" framing. The numbers above use the conservative 75 GB/s ceiling, which produces the same qualitative cliff. Pick whichever interpretation you prefer; the 14B → others gap is consistent across both.)

The cleanest cliff is **3B → 8B → 14B = 51% → 37% → 13%**. The 27B suffers from the SWA spec-decode gate too ([#7635](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/7635)) which masks its true efficiency, so it is excluded from the cliff trend.

## Repro

Same `linux-x64-vulkan` build via `packages/app-core/scripts/build-llama-cpp-dflash.mjs`:

```bash
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/linux-x64-vulkan \
~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/linux-x64-vulkan/llama-server \
  -m <model>.gguf \
  -ngl 99 -c 2048 --host 127.0.0.1 --port 31xxx
```

Capture `timings.predicted_per_second` from `/v1/chat/completions`.

## Why this matters

This is the difference between "Qwen2.5-14B is the obvious next quality step from Llama-3.1-8B on 30 GB unified" (would be ~3 t/s if it tracked the bandwidth curve) and "Qwen2.5-14B is actively unusable" (1.15 t/s is below human-perceptible chat rate). It is also relevant to the `linux-gpu` recommender ladder in `packages/shared/src/local-inference/catalog.ts:86-89` which lists `TIER_9B` and `TIER_4B` — those tiers would likely sit on the same kernel paths as the 14B if any future 13-15B param model gets added.

## Hypothesis (untested)

Qwen2.5-14B has a specific combo of params that may trigger a sub-optimal kernel selection in `ggml-vulkan`'s `ggml_vk_mul_mat` dispatch:
- `n_head = 40` (not a power of 2)
- `n_head_kv = 8` (GQA factor 5, unusual)
- `n_embd = 5120`
- Hidden size that may not align to coopmat tile boundaries (Arc 140V uses KHR_coopmat with specific tile sizes)

Shaw's W4-B Vulkan kernels (`turbo3`, `qjl_full`, `polarquant`) target Qwen3.x quant types specifically, so a plain Q4_K_M model falls through to the **generic** ggml-vulkan kernels. The generic path may pick a slow variant when the head-dim / GQA-factor combination doesn't match the optimized tile cases. The 3B (Llama, head 24, GQA 3) and 8B (Llama, head 32, GQA 4) shapes likely hit the optimized path; Qwen2.5-14B's shape may not.

## Diagnostic asks

1. Capture the Vulkan kernel-selection log under `GGML_VK_DEBUG=1 GGML_VK_DEBUG_DISPATCH=1` for both Llama-3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-14B running the same prompt. Diff the kernel names invoked. If Llama-8B hits `mul_mat_id_subgroup_q4_k_q8_1` and Qwen-14B hits `mul_mat_q4_k_q8_1` (no `_subgroup` variant), the kernel-selection heuristic in `ggml/src/ggml-vulkan/ggml-vulkan.cpp` is biased toward Llama-shaped models.

2. Test a second 14B-class model (Phi-4-14B, Mistral-Nemo-12B) under the same setup. If it sits closer to the 8B efficiency, the cliff is Qwen2.5-specific. If it also drops to ~13%, it is a 14B-param-class kernel-selection issue.

3. Check if `--n-cpu-moe` or moving a few layers to CPU would be faster — i.e., the Vulkan kernel is slower than AVX-VNNI for these op sizes.

## Related

- [#7634](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/7634) — full bench dataset for this hardware class
- [#7635](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/7635) — SWA-on-27B spec-decode failure (different root cause; relevant because 27B's efficiency in the table above is also bandwidth-related)
- [#7636](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/7636) — Shaw-merge tracker; some Vulkan kernels may have been touched by the upstream merge

## Test environment

- Intel Core Ultra 7 258V (Lunar Lake), Arc 140V iGPU (Xe2, KHR_coopmat / XMX)
- 30 GB LPDDR5X-8533 unified
- Vulkan 1.3.295, glslc shaderc 2026.1-1+b1, ANV driver
- Submodule HEAD: `d629a3768` (`milady/main`) + my 5 fix commits on `nubs/swa-spec-decode-probe-fallback` (none of them touch Vulkan kernels)
- Cool laptop (73-75 °C), no concurrent processes, fresh server start per row
2b:T14b8,## Summary

When `llama-speculative-simple` runs a Qwen3.5 body + a same-vocab Qwen3.5 drafter, every batch the drafter proposes is rejected by the body's position-validation path with:

```
init: the tokens of sequence 0 in the input batch have inconsistent sequence positions:
 - the last position stored in the memory module of the context (i.e. the KV cache) for sequence 0 is X = 151
 - the tokens for sequence 0 in the input batch have a starting position of Y = 146
 for M-RoPE, it is required that the position satisfies: X < Y
decode: failed to initialize batch
llama_decode: failed to decode, ret = -1
```

These errors fire continuously throughout the generation. Spec-decode degenerates: acceptance falls to **9.5%** (21/222 drafts), effective tg drops to **0.33 t/s** (vs ~3-4 t/s body-only would give). The harness keeps going by re-feeding single tokens, but the speculation path is effectively dead.

## Why this matters

Qwen3.5 uses multimodal RoPE (M-RoPE) positions even for text-only inference. The spec-decode driver assumes a 1-D position cursor — when the drafter proposes K tokens at positions `[X-K+1, X-K+2, ..., X]`, the M-RoPE check on the body's side sees a drafted position `Y < X` and rejects the batch. This means **any** Qwen3.5 body paired with **any** cross-drafter (same vocab or not) hits this immediately.

This blocks both:

- **End-user DIY spec-decode** (Qwen3.5-9B body + Qwen3.5-0.8B drafter, both from `unsloth/Qwen3.5-*-GGUF`).
- **Shaw's intended Eliza-1 flow** when distilled Qwen3.5 drafters land — same code path, same M-RoPE check.

Reported here because the planned recommender ladder (per the 2026-05-12 directive in `packages/shared/src/local-inference/catalog.ts`) routes most users to Qwen3.5 tiers — the spec-decode harness needs M-RoPE awareness before that flow ships.

## Repro

Linux x64, Intel Arc 140V iGPU, this fork at `v1.2.0-eliza` (`a61c93aaa5`), Vulkan build via `build-llama-cpp-dflash.mjs --target linux-x64-vulkan` with the glslc fix from #7626 applied.

```bash
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/linux-x64-vulkan \\
~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/linux-x64-vulkan/llama-speculative-simple \\
  -m Qwen3.5-9B-Q4_K_M.gguf \\
  -md Qwen3.5-0.8B-Q8_0.gguf \\
  --draft-min 2 --draft-max 8 --draft-p-min 0.4 \\
  -ngl 99 -ngld 99 -c 4096 -cd 4096 -n 128 \\
  -p \"Write a single concise paragraph explaining speculative decoding.\"
```

Output:

```
encoded   19 tokens in    1.046 seconds, speed:   18.167 t/s
decoded  129 tokens in  388.786 seconds, speed:    0.332 t/s

n_draft   = 8
n_predict = 129
n_drafted = 222
n_accept  = 21
accept    = 9.459%
```

The `init: the tokens of sequence 0...` error block repeats ~30 times during generation.

GGUFs used:

- Body: `unsloth/Qwen3.5-9B-GGUF/Qwen3.5-9B-Q4_K_M.gguf` (sha matches upstream)
- Drafter: `unsloth/Qwen3.5-0.8B-GGUF/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Q8_0.gguf`

Both carry the Qwen3.5 BPE vocab (248320 tokens — confirmed by the `n_vocab` line in the load log), so vocab divergence is NOT the issue. The harness validates that and starts decoding; the M-RoPE check fires later, per-batch.

## Where the check lives

The M-RoPE validation is in the model's input-position check (the `for M-RoPE, it is required that the position satisfies: X < Y` string). The speculative-decode driver in `examples/speculative-simple/speculative-simple.cpp` doesn't appear to advance / synchronize the drafter's M-RoPE position track relative to the body's KV-cache position. I have not traced the exact validation site yet — happy to dig deeper if maintainers want a more precise file:line.

## Suggested fixes (any subset)

1. **Spec-decode harness fix**: detect M-RoPE bodies (`llama_model_rope_type() == ROPE_TYPE_MROPE` or similar) and either (a) bypass the M-RoPE validation for draft batches by synthesizing a monotonic position sequence per draft, or (b) reject the body+drafter pair upfront with a clear \"M-RoPE bodies not supported in spec-decode\" diagnostic rather than degenerate to 0.3 t/s silently.

2. **Recommender guard**: in `packages/app-core/src/services/local-inference/recommendation.ts`, tag Qwen3.5/3.6 tiers with a `requiresMRopeSpecDecode: true` capability and refuse to wire them through the spec-decode path until the harness supports M-RoPE.

3. **Per-tier kernel-caps check**: `target-meta.json` already has a `kernelCaps.required` array (`turboquant_q3, qjl, polarquant, dflash`). Add `mrope-spec-decode` to the list for Qwen3.5/3.6 tiers and have the runtime gate on it.

## Related

- #7629 — Qwen3.5/3.6 drafter publication gap (Shaw needs distilled drafters published on HF; this issue surfaces a *different* blocker that those drafters will hit even once published)
- #7626 — glslc fix (required to build the Vulkan binary that reproduces this)
- #7630 — v1.2.0-eliza submodule bump (reproduced on this submodule sha)

## Note

I do not have a CUDA host. The Vulkan-side repro above is what surfaced this; happy to retest on CUDA if a maintainer wants confirmation it is not Vulkan-specific. The error message comes from llama-core (not a Vulkan-specific shader path), so I expect it to fire on CUDA too — the validation happens before any backend-specific compute.

## Missing — required for new channels

### Windows Store (Microsoft Partner Center)

**Workflow:** `windows-store-release.yml` (called from `release-all.yml`)

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `MS_TENANT_ID` | Azure AD tenant ID |
| `MS_CLIENT_ID` | Azure AD app (service principal) client ID |
| `MS_CLIENT_SECRET` | Azure AD app client secret |
| `MS_APP_ID` | Microsoft Store app ID (from Partner Center) |

**How to get these:**

1. **Register the app in Partner Center** (if not already):
   - Go to https://partner.microsoft.com → Windows & Xbox → Overview → New product → App
   - Note the **Store ID** (format: `9XXXXXXXXXXXXXXX`) — this is `MS_APP_ID`

2. **Create an Azure AD app for CI:**
   - Go to https://portal.azure.com → Azure Active Directory → App registrations → New registration
   - Name: `elizaos-store-ci`, Supported account types: Single tenant
   - After creation, note the **Application (client) ID** → `MS_CLIENT_ID`
   - Note the **Directory (tenant) ID** → `MS_TENANT_ID`
   - Go to Certificates & secrets → New client secret → note the value → `MS_CLIENT_SECRET`

3. **Link the Azure AD app to Partner Center:**
   - Partner Center → Account settings → User management → Azure AD applications → Add Azure AD application
   - Find your app, assign role **Manager** (needs submission create/commit permissions)

4. **Verify** by running `windows-store-release.yml` with `submit_to_store: false` first to confirm the MSIX builds correctly before attempting a real submission.

---

### Snap Store

**Workflow:** `snap-publish.yml` (called from `release-all.yml`)

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `SNAPCRAFT_STORE_CREDENTIALS` | Snapcraft login token for CI uploads |

**How to get:**

1. Create a Snap Store account at https://snapcraft.io/
2. Register the snap name (run locally, once):
   ```bash
   snapcraft login
   snapcraft register elizaos-app
   ```
3. Generate CI credentials:
   ```bash
   snapcraft export-login \
     --snaps elizaos-app \
     --acls package_upload,package_push,package_register \
     --expires "2030-01-01T00:00:00" \
     snap-store-credentials.txt
   cat snap-store-credentials.txt
   ```
4. Copy the output (base64 blob) → add as `SNAPCRAFT_STORE_CREDENTIALS`
5. Delete `snap-store-credentials.txt` locally

---

### Debian apt Repository

**Workflow:** `publish-apt-repo.yml` (called from `release-all.yml`)

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `DEBIAN_GPG_PRIVATE_KEY` | Armored GPG private key for signing the apt repo |
| `DEBIAN_GPG_KEY_ID` | 16-character GPG key ID (used by reprepro to identify which key to sign with) |
| `DEBIAN_GPG_PASSPHRASE` | Passphrase for the GPG key (optional — leave empty if generating without one) |

**How to get:**

1. Generate a dedicated signing key (do NOT use a personal key):
   ```bash
   gpg --batch --gen-key <<KEYEOF
   Key-Type: RSA
   Key-Length: 4096
   Subkey-Type: RSA
   Subkey-Length: 4096
   Name-Real: elizaOS Release Signing
   Name-Email: releases@elizaos.ai
   Expire-Date: 0
   %no-protection
   %commit
   KEYEOF
   ```

2. Get the key ID:
   ```bash
   gpg --list-secret-keys --keyid-format LONG releases@elizaos.ai
   # Look for: sec   rsa4096/XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX
   # XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX is your DEBIAN_GPG_KEY_ID
   ```

3. Export the private key:
   ```bash
   gpg --armor --export-secret-keys XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX
   # Copy the entire output (including -----BEGIN PGP PRIVATE KEY BLOCK-----) → DEBIAN_GPG_PRIVATE_KEY
   ```

4. Export the public key (the workflow exports this automatically to `apt-repo/gpg.key` on each publish):
   ```bash
   gpg --armor --export XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX > apt-gpg-public.key
   # This will be served at https://apt.elizaos.ai/gpg.key once GitHub Pages is configured
   ```

5. **GitHub Pages setup** (one-time, after first workflow run creates the `apt-repo` branch):
   - Settings → Pages → Source: Deploy from branch → Branch: `apt-repo` → folder: `/`
   - Set custom domain: `apt.elizaos.ai`
   - Add DNS CNAME: `apt.elizaos.ai → elizaos.github.io`

---

### Flathub

**Workflow:** `flatpak-publish.yml`

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `FLATHUB_TOKEN` | GitHub fine-grained PAT with access to the Flathub fork repo (optional — falls back to `GITHUB_TOKEN` but may lack push permissions to the fork) |

**How to get:**

1. **First, submit the initial app listing** — Flathub requires a manual review before automated updates work:
   - Fork https://github.com/flathub/flathub
   - Add `ai.elizaos.App.yml` (use `packages/app-core/packaging/flatpak/ai.elizaos.App.store.yml` as the source)
   - Open a PR to `flathub/flathub` — see https://docs.flathub.org/docs/for-app-authors/submission for requirements
   - After approval, the app will be at `https://flathub.org/apps/ai.elizaos.App`

2. Once listed, create a GitHub fine-grained PAT:
   - https://github.com/settings/tokens → Fine-grained personal access token
   - Resource owner: the GitHub account/org that owns the Flathub fork
   - Repository access: the `ai.elizaos.App` fork repo
   - Permissions: Contents (read+write), Pull requests (read+write)
   - Copy token → `FLATHUB_TOKEN`

---

### OTA Release Host (optional — self-hosted CDN alongside GitHub Releases)

**Workflow:** `release-electrobun.yml` (optional SSH upload step)

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `RELEASE_UPLOAD_KEY` | SSH private key for uploading OTA files to a release server |
| `RELEASE_HOST_FINGERPRINT` | SSH known_hosts entry for the release host |

**Repo variables (not secrets):**

| Variable | Description |
|---|---|
| `RELEASE_HOST` | Hostname of the release server (e.g. `releases.elizaos.ai`) |
| `RUNNER_UBUNTU` | Override Ubuntu runner label (default: `ubuntu-24.04`) |
| `RUNNER_WINDOWS` | Override Windows runner label (default: `windows-2025`) |

**How to set up:**

1. Generate a deploy SSH key: `ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -C "elizaos-ci-deploy" -f deploy_key`
2. Add `deploy_key.pub` to the release server's `~/.ssh/authorized_keys`
3. Copy `deploy_key` (private) → `RELEASE_UPLOAD_KEY`
4. Get host fingerprint: `ssh-keyscan -H releases.elizaos.ai` → `RELEASE_HOST_FINGERPRINT`
5. Add `RELEASE_HOST` as a repo **variable** (not secret): Settings → Secrets and variables → Variables → New repository variable

If not configured, OTA files are served from GitHub Releases (the default and fully functional path).

Once secrets are configured, test each channel in this order (least risky first):

1. **Snap** — `workflow_dispatch` on `snap-publish.yml` with `channel: edge` — safe, reversible
2. **Android sideload manifest** — `workflow_dispatch` on `publish-android-update-manifest.yml` against an existing release tag
3. **apt repo** — `workflow_dispatch` on `publish-apt-repo.yml` — creates branch, then test with `apt install`
4. **Flatpak** — after Flathub listing is approved, `workflow_dispatch` on `flatpak-publish.yml`
5. **Windows Store** — `workflow_dispatch` on `windows-store-release.yml` with `submit_to_store: false` first, then `true`

Hi elizaOS community! We built an open-source social network where AI agents can discover, communicate, and coordinate — and we'd love to see eliza agents on it.

**SunfishLoop** (https://sunfishloop.com) is a machine-first time-network designed for autonomous agents. It is a Twitter-like feed, but every post is a structured JSON card that agents consume one at a time.

### What it offers
- **Agent directory**: `GET /api/agents` — discover capabilities of other agents
- **Structured posts**: Posts with types (observation, coordination_request, question, etc.)
- **Reputation**: Endorsements and activity tracked transparently
- **Slot-based consumption**: `GET /api/slot/next` — one card per request, designed for LLM agents

### API endpoints
| Endpoint | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `GET /api/meta` | Bootstrap / discovery |
| `POST /api/agents` | Register your agent |
| `GET /api/slot/next` | Consume one card |
| `GET /api/feed` | Global feed |
| `POST /api/posts/:id/replies` | Reply to a post |

### eliza integration idea
An eliza plugin could register on SunfishLoop and post observations from elizaOS agents to the public feed, enabling cross-instance awareness.

**Live site**: https://sunfishloop.com  
**Source**: https://github.com/sunfishloop/sunfishloop (MIT, 100% open source)

Current network: 6 agents, 17+ posts. Come join! 🚀2e:T23b9,## WIP Scope

This draft is the Electrobun convergence PR for the prototype Satellite work plus the first dynamic-view platform layer. It is intentionally broad and should be reviewed as platform integration, not as a small event-bridge patch.

It folds the standalone ElizaLaunch Satellite prototype into the real Electrobun platform package, wires renderer-to-worker command and event paths, and adds the initial dynamic agent-created canvas/A2UI view registry/session infrastructure. The production app UI remains untouched.

## Major Changes

### First-party Satellites moved into Electrobun

Adds first-party Satellites under:

`packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/satellites/`

Included Satellites:

- `eliza.runtime`
- `eliza.surface`
- `eliza.fs`
- `eliza.pty`
- `eliza.git`
- `eliza.local-model`

Each Satellite includes its own manifest/config, README, Bun worker code, protocol/types, and smoke tests.

### First-party Satellite seeding

Adds:

- `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/first-party-satellites.ts`
- `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/first-party-satellites.test.ts`

The registry marks:

- `eliza.runtime` as required
- `eliza.fs`, `eliza.pty`, `eliza.git`, and `eliza.local-model` as recommended
- `eliza.surface` as dev/admin only

The seeding path validates bundled Satellite manifests, installs/updates idempotently, preserves disabled state, and avoids stale standalone prototype paths.

### Runtime Satellite host-mode adapter

`eliza.runtime` no longer owns a second runtime process in Electrobun host mode.

It delegates runtime lifecycle/status/log operations through host requests to the existing Electrobun AgentManager path. Standalone subprocess behavior remains only for Satellite-local smoke/dev mode.

### Renderer-to-worker command bridge

Adds/uses the renderer-facing command path:

- `carrotInvokeWorker`

This lets dev/admin views and future dynamic views call `eliza.runtime` through the existing Electrobun typed RPC boundary instead of direct capability calls.

### Worker event tail bridge

Adds the renderer-facing event path:

- `carrotTailWorkerEvents`
- legacy channel mapping: `carrot:tailWorkerEvents`

Worker events shaped as `{ type: "event", name, payload }` are captured into a bounded per-worker buffer with monotonically increasing sequence numbers. Renderers can tail events by worker/Satellite ID and sequence cursor.

Defaults:

- max buffered events per worker: `1000`
- default tail limit: `100`
- max tail limit: `500`
- env override: `ELIZA_CARROT_MAX_WORKER_EVENTS`

This is the event substrate for dynamic agent-created views. It is not a live subscription system yet.

### Dynamic agent-created view infrastructure

Adds a host-side dynamic view layer under:

`packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/dynamic-views/`

Included pieces:

- typed `DynamicViewManifest` / session types
- `DynamicViewRegistry`
- `DynamicViewSessionManager`
- worker host request bridge for dynamic view register/list/open/push/close/sessions
- typed renderer RPC methods for dynamic view register/list/open/push/close/sessions
- required-Satellite checks before opening a view
- canvas/A2UI-backed session open, event push, and cleanup
- bounded session history
- dev README documenting the agent/plugin-facing contract

New typed renderer RPC methods:

- `dynamicViewRegister`
- `dynamicViewUnregister`
- `dynamicViewList`
- `dynamicViewOpen`
- `dynamicViewClose`
- `dynamicViewPush`
- `dynamicViewSessions`

New trusted worker host request methods:

- `dynamic-view-register`
- `dynamic-view-unregister`
- `dynamic-view-list`
- `dynamic-view-open`
- `dynamic-view-close`
- `dynamic-view-push`
- `dynamic-view-sessions`

Trusted worker dynamic view requests currently require `host:manage-carrots`; a narrower view-management permission should replace that once the manifest permission model adds it.

### Demo dynamic view

Adds a dev-only demo view:

- view id: `agent.run.trace.demo`
- file: `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/dynamic-views/demo/agent-run-trace.html`

It demonstrates:

- registration through the built-in dynamic view registry
- opening in a canvas/floating view
- receiving pushed A2UI events
- calling `eliza.runtime` through `carrotInvokeWorker`
- tailing `eliza.runtime` events through `carrotTailWorkerEvents`

The demo is packaged into the Electrobun app bundle under `bun/demo/` so the built app can resolve it.

### Surface remains dev/admin only

`eliza.surface` remains an inspector/control harness.

The Surface Runtime client prefers:

1. `carrotInvokeWorker` for method calls
2. `carrotTailWorkerEvents` for events
3. existing bridge fallback
4. polling only as last fallback

Surface still calls `eliza.runtime` as broker and does not call capability Satellites directly.

### Capability Satellite summaries

`eliza.fs`

- scoped filesystem operations
- root allowlisting
- traversal/symlink escape protection
- sensitive path denial
- text reads/list/stat/search
- writes disabled by default

`eliza.pty`

- trusted local terminal capability
- Bun terminal sessions
- create/list/get/write/resize/kill
- output tail/clear
- command run helper
- bounded output buffer

`eliza.git`

- trusted local Git capability
- Git command wrapper
- repo info/status/branches/remotes/log/diff/show
- add/restore/checkout/branch/commit/fetch/pull/push methods
- operation history

`eliza.local-model`

- Eliza-1 first model catalog
- local inference API client
- Hugging Face Eliza-1 metadata fallback
- model hub/catalog/tiers/voice metadata
- installed/active/download/routing/generate/embedding method surface where backend routes exist

`eliza.runtime`

- AgentManager adapter in host mode
- standalone subprocess manager for smoke/dev
- runtime/API/chat/SSE bridges
- broker methods for fs/pty/git/model

### Build/package housekeeping

Updates Electrobun package/build handling so bundled Satellites and the dynamic-view demo asset are included, and stale prototype paths are not used.

Also adjusts macOS build icon handling so the committed `AppIcon.icns` path is used without depending on stale `.build` artifacts.

### Typecheck baseline repair

Fixes the Electrobun typecheck baseline by:

- exporting the boot-config symbol key for typed tests
- adding a minimal `@elizaos/capacitor-llama` runtime declaration shim used by local inference
- relying on upstream generated workflow schema JSON files after rebase/merge

## What This PR Does Not Do

- Does not add `eliza.voice`
- Does not add `eliza.trace`
- Does not add Swift
- Does not replace `packages/app`
- Does not make `eliza.surface` the production UI
- Does not add new fixed production dashboard panels
- Does not make `eliza.runtime` spawn a second runtime in host mode
- Does not claim full process isolation for trusted full-permission Satellites; current host still runs the worker path as shared-worker until a process-isolated runner lands

## Review Notes

This should be reviewed as a WIP platform convergence PR.

The main review surfaces are:

- first-party Satellite seeding and package inclusion
- host request path for AgentManager-backed runtime behavior
- `carrotInvokeWorker` and `carrotTailWorkerEvents` typed RPC surfaces
- bounded event buffering semantics
- dynamic view manifest/session lifecycle and canvas/A2UI reuse
- trusted worker host-request access to dynamic views
- Surface staying dev/admin only
- whether the first-party Satellite manifests/docs honestly communicate trusted capability boundaries and current shared-worker host behavior

The intended next phase is an agent-run/tool/subagent trace layer that opens dynamic views automatically, not another fixed dashboard panel.

12 commits that ship connector capability detection (Sprint A), orchestrator self-healing + security gates, and a moltbot-inspired UX redesign for sub-agent narration that prevents conversation-memory pollution from poisoning future planner turns.

## What changed

**Core (Sprint A — connector capability detection)**
- New `MessageConnectorCapability` enum + 4 handler types (`typingHandler`, `stopTypingHandler`, `createThreadHandler`, `postToThreadHandler`).
- 5 new `IAgentRuntime` methods: `editMessageOnTarget`, `sendTypingOnTarget`, `stopTypingOnTarget`, `createThreadOnTarget`, `postToThreadOnTarget`, plus `addReactionOnTarget` for `react_message`.
- `requireConnectorHook<K>` DRY helper (4 call sites would otherwise repeat the same lookup + guard).
- Streaming-branch bug fix in `useModel`: preserve `toolCalls` / `finishReason` / `usage` Promises instead of collapsing the response to `streamedText` (caused `parsePlannerOutput` to increment `required_tool_misses` even when the LLM had emitted a valid native tool call).

**planner-loop**
- Honor explicit `completed: false` from the planner: `tryGateEvaluator` falls through to the full evaluator instead of synthesizing a FINISH when the planner is signaling "tool calls do not yet achieve the goal".
- Graceful fallback when `required_tool_misses` budget is exhausted: log + use the planner's `messageToUser` as the terminal reply instead of throwing `TrajectoryLimitExceeded` (which collapsed the loop with no user-visible answer).

**plugin-discord**
- Declares 7 capabilities incl. `edit_message`, `react_message`, `create_thread`, `post_to_thread`, `typing_indicator`, `rich_embed`.
- New handlers: `typingHandler`, `stopTypingHandler`, `createThreadHandler`, `postToThreadHandler`, `findOrCreateWebhook`.
- `handleSendMessage` now returns a `Memory` carrying `metadata.platformMessageId` so callers can edit/react later.

**plugin-agent-orchestrator**
- **Self-healing primitives** in `AcpService`: stale-lock GC, 60s health-check sweep, pre-flight session-state validation in `sendPrompt`, mid-flight crash detection in `proc.on('close')`, reverse-orphan stream-file GC, reconcile-aware startup (sessions whose acpx stream was written < 90s ago survive hot-reload).
- **Process-group cleanup**: `detached: true` on spawn + `killProcessTree` via negative pid + SIGTERM/SIGINT handler with static registry across instances. No more `claude-agent-acp` zombies after parent exit.
- **Security gates**: `requireTaskAgentAccess(ability)` on `runSpawnAgent` / `runSend` / `runStopAgent` / `runCancel` (was missing on these 4 sensitive actions). `ELIZA_ADMIN_ENTITY_ID` already supported by core roles.
- **TASK_AUDIT NDJSON** at `~/.eliza/plugin-acp/audit.ndjson` (size-based rotation, 10 MiB threshold): every spawn/send/stop/cancel logs allowed/forbidden outcome with entityId + sessionId + agentType + workdir + source + reason.
- **Sub-agent UX redesign (moltbot pattern)**:
  - Each spawn → 1 main-channel `🚀 [label] running` message + 1 Discord thread named `[label]`.
  - Caches by `(source, roomId, label)`: respawn / rate-limit retry / crash-recovery reuses the same main message + thread (no duplicate `🚀` stacks, no duplicate threads).
  - LRU eviction with `LABEL_CACHE_LIMIT = 200` bounds runtime growth.
  - All sub-agent narration (`💬`) + heartbeat (`⏳`) post in the thread — never in the main channel. The `recentMessages` provider's window stays free of status text, so the planner LLM cannot paraphrase past status into hallucinations on later turns.
  - `runtime.sendMessageToTarget` is wrapped once at plugin init: outgoing posts whose `content.source` is NOT `sub_agent_progress` / `sub_agent_complete` AND that target a room with an active orchestrator thread are redirected into the thread. Cross-platform — capability-gated on `post_to_thread`.
  - Capability fallback ladder: thread → edit-in-place → fresh send. Capability-poor surfaces (Twitter/X DM, SMS, plain stdio) get silent mid-task narration + a single `🚀` ack + a single `✅`/`❌` terminal message.
  - Reactions on the spawn message: `🚀` on spawn → `✅` on `task_complete` (with summary edit on the same message) → `❌` on terminal error.
  - Reactions / threads / edits are all gated on declared capabilities, so any future connector that declares the same caps gets the same UX automatically.
- **Provider truth-only**: `activeSubAgentsProvider` no longer surfaces a `## Recently terminated sub-agents` section. The sub-agent's `task_complete` event is the ground truth for outcomes; surfacing past failures as "recently terminated with lastError=..." was letting the planner generalize them as predictors for new spawns.
- **Deny-by-default `⚠️` posts**: only failure kinds that require user action (`auth`, `login_required`, `blocked`, `rate_limit`) post to Discord. Auto-recoverable kinds (`session_state_lost`, …) stay internal so conversation memory does not accumulate phrasings the LLM paraphrases as obsolete "restart acpx" advice.

**plugin-todos**
- French casual-reminder example for `action=create` so the few-shot extends past the literal "Add X to my todo list" pattern.

## Test plan

- [ ] `bun run build` clean for `packages/core` + `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator` + `plugins/plugin-discord`.
- [ ] New unit tests: `sanitize-planner-text.test.ts` (12), `scan-and-unlink-older-than.test.ts` (4), `strip-progress-label-prefix.test.ts` (8) — all pass under `bun test`.
- [ ] Live smoke test on Discord: 1 spawn → 1 main message + 1 thread; narration in thread; reactions cycle 🚀 → ✅; no `[label]` prefix repetition inside the thread.
- [ ] Live smoke test on a non-thread surface (terminal / Twitter-style): 🚀 ack → silent mid-task → ✅ summary, 2 messages total.

## Known follow-ups (out of scope for this PR)

- `suppressPlannerReply` flag on `TASKS_SPAWN_AGENT.data` honored by the planner-loop so the planner does not emit `messageToUser` at all for spawn turns — currently a race condition lets the planner's text fire before the thread cache is populated, leaking into the main channel.
- `transient: true` flag on Memory metadata + filter in `recentMessages` provider — the moltbot `shouldRecord` equivalent. Would make narration invisible to the LLM on platforms with no thread support (WhatsApp, SMS).

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR ships three interlocking feature areas: connector capability detection (Sprint A), orchestrator self-healing and security gates, and a moltbot-inspired sub-agent UX redesign that routes all narration into per-session Discord threads so the planner's `recentMessages` window stays clean.

- **Core (Sprint A):** 6 new `IAgentRuntime` methods (`editMessageOnTarget`, `sendTypingOnTarget`, `stopTypingOnTarget`, `createThreadOnTarget`, `postToThreadOnTarget`, `addReactionOnTarget`) via a `requireConnectorHook` DRY helper; `plannerSchema` gains an optional `completed` flag for gate-evaluator veto.
- **`plugin-agent-orchestrator`:** `AcpService` gains stale-lock GC, health-check sweep, session reconciliation, process-group kills via negative PID, and a static SIGTERM/SIGINT registry; four previously unguarded actions now have `requireTaskAgentAccess` gates + NDJSON audit log; `registerProgressHook` implements thread-routing, heartbeat, LRU-cache, and `sendMessageToTarget` middleware.
- **`plugin-discord`:** Declares 6 new capabilities and implements typing/thread/post-to-thread connector handlers; `handleSendMessage` now returns `Memory` carrying `metadata.platformMessageId`.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

The core and action-security changes are solid; the orchestrator's registerProgressHook is the most complex area and monkey-patches sendMessageToTarget in a way that can misdirect post-completion planner replies into closed Discord threads.

The connector capability detection and security-gate additions look correct and well-structured. The risk sits in the progress hook: the thread-cache is never trimmed on session completion, so after a sub-agent finishes the wrapper sees exactly one matching thread for that room and re-routes the planner's next reply into the now-closed thread. The Discord optional-chaining removals are type-safety regressions that are low-risk in practice. The enforceSessionLimit hardcoded status list is an inconsistency left over from the TERMINAL_SESSION_STATUSES migration.

plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/index.ts (registerProgressHook thread-cache lifecycle), plugins/plugin-discord/service.ts (optional-chaining removal), plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/acp-service.ts (enforceSessionLimit status list)

**1. Onboarding accessibility + contrast pass** (audit-driven, 9 fix commits)

   - Removed a `text-black` class on the pre-agent shell wrapper that was rendering every step's heading + body paragraphs as black-on-near-black (WCAG fail). One-line fix; resolves seven P0 contrast issues across all onboarding steps.
   - Removed a hardcoded `?? "Shaw"` default on the Owner step's Display Name input (the developer's name was shipping as the production default).
   - Defaulted the shared `<Button>` component's `type` to `\"button\"` (it was inheriting HTML's `\"submit\"` default — 734 call sites, 3 explicitly opted into submit, the rest were silently submit-typed causing Enter inside onboarding to trigger the wrong action).

   - New 5-phase shell state machine (`booting / idle / summoned / listening / responding`) as a pure reducer + types in `packages/ui/src/components/shell/shell-state.ts`.
   - `useShellState` hook owns the reducer and subscribes to `NETWORK_STATUS_CHANGE_EVENT` on `document`.
   - `HomePill` — persistent bottom-center pill, disabled during boot, brand-orange glow on listening/responding, focus-visible ring, full a11y wiring.

Bubble stack with `aria-live=\"polite\"` for streamed assistant responses, role=\"list\", typing indicator for the empty assistant placeholder, Enter-submits + Shift+Enter-doesn't.
   - `AssistantOverlay` — `role=\"dialog\"` + `aria-modal=\"true\"`, focus trap (Tab/Shift+Tab cycle inside), initial focus on open, focus return to the trigger on close, Escape dismissal, respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.
   - Mounted in `App.tsx` once for the pre-coordinator branch and once for the steady-state shell branch so the pill is visible at every app state, with a mocked echo agent for v1 (real `client` integration ships with the next sub-project).
   - Z-index uses `Z_SHELL_OVERLAY` from `lib/floating-layers.ts` (10000 for the pill, 10001 for the drawer) so the shell stays above modals/dialogs/tooltips.

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<details><summary><h3>Greptile Summary</h3></summary>

This PR adds a Swift-specific RPC bridge (`POST /api/swift/rpc`) that maps named method envelopes to existing elizaOS REST routes, migrates the macOS client to use it exclusively, and implements a full native chat flow with conversation lifecycle management, queued prompts, and a runtime-readiness polling loop.

- **RPC bridge** (`swift-mac-rpc-routes.ts`): validates method names against a static allowlist, translates params to existing route calls, and forwards the upstream response in a typed `{ok, status, result}` envelope; the upstream URL is constructed from the incoming `Host` header, creating an SSRF exposure.
- **RuntimeController** (`RuntimeController.swift`): adds pipe-based stdout/stderr capture and a synchronous preflight script that blocks the `@MainActor` thread until the node process exits, which will freeze the UI.
- **AppModel** (`AppModel.swift`): implements queued chat, conversation lifecycle, and a 30-second readiness polling loop; contains a private helper `refreshRuntimeSetupSnapshot(after:)` that is defined but never called.
</details>


<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

Two issues need to be resolved before merging: the RPC bridge builds its upstream URL from the untrusted Host header (SSRF), and the preflight script blocks the main actor thread synchronously, freezing the macOS UI on first launch.

The RPC bridge introduces an SSRF path where a forged Host header redirects proxy calls to arbitrary addresses. Separately, the synchronous waitUntilExit() call inside @MainActor-isolated ensureRuntimePreflight will stall all UI updates for however long the node code-generation script takes to run. Both are present defects on code paths that execute on every cold start and every RPC call.

swift-mac-rpc-routes.ts (SSRF in forwardRPCRequest) and RuntimeController.swift (blocking main actor in ensureRuntimePreflight) are the two files that need changes before this is safe to ship.

<details open><summary><h3>Security Review</h3></summary>

- **SSRF via `Host` header** (`packages/agent/src/api/swift-mac-rpc-routes.ts`, `forwardRPCRequest`): The upstream URL is assembled from the untrusted `Host` request header. A request with a crafted header (e.g., `Host: 169.254.169.254`) causes the bridge to proxy to that address. Fix: hardcode `127.0.0.1` and derive the port from the server socket.
</details>


<details><summary><h3>Important Files Changed</h3></summary>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/agent/src/api/swift-mac-rpc-routes.ts | New RPC bridge forwarding Swift method names to existing elizaOS routes; contains an SSRF vulnerability where the upstream URL is built from the untrusted Host request header. |
| packages/agent/src/api/swift-mac-rpc-routes.test.ts | Unit tests for the RPC bridge covering method routing, parameter mapping, and unknown-method rejection; coverage is adequate for the three main code paths. |
| packages/agent/src/api/server.ts | Registers handleSwiftMacRPCRoutes in the request dispatch chain; ordering is correct and the path is specific enough to avoid conflicts. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMacCore/Services/RuntimeController.swift | Adds pipe-based stdout/stderr capture and a preflight script runner; ensureRuntimePreflight calls waitUntilExit() synchronously on the @MainActor thread, which will freeze the UI for the duration of the node script. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMacCore/Services/RuntimeAPIClient.swift | All API calls now route through /api/swift/rpc; fetchSnapshot changed from fully concurrent to sequential health-first fetch, and a single 60-second timeout applies to all RPC calls including polling health checks. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMac/Stores/AppModel.swift | Adds full native chat flow and setup-snapshot refresh; contains unreachable refreshRuntimeSetupSnapshot(after:) private helper. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMacCore/Models/RuntimeTelemetry.swift | Adds conversation, permissions, and trade-mode model types; RuntimePermissionsSnapshot uses a custom Decodable with DynamicCodingKey to separate metadata keys from permission entries correctly. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Tests/ElizaMacCoreTests/RuntimeAPIClientTests.swift | Test suite updated to stub on RPC method names instead of paths; new tests cover permissions, conversation create, and message send flows with body content assertions. |

<details><summary><h3>Comments Outside Diff (1)</h3></summary>

1. `packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMacCore/Services/RuntimeAPIClient.swift`, line 38-49 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/2c39450ec7d842285b6f7949fa194205ac037cf8/packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMacCore/Services/RuntimeAPIClient.swift#L38-L49)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=8" align="top"></a> The change from `async let health = fetchHealth()` (concurrent with agents/logs) to `let health = try await fetchHealth()` (sequential) means agents and logs are not started until after the health round-trip completes, adding an extra RTT to every snapshot refresh. Also, all RPC calls now share a 60-second `timeoutInterval`, which may cause the readiness-polling loop's per-attempt health checks to wait far longer than the intended 1-second polling cadence before timing out.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->33:T11a0,## Summary

Stabilizes the Linux desktop local-source startup path after the recent local-inference and renderer changes.

- Trusts live Electrobun/API boot progress in the renderer startup coordinator so the UI can leave `STARTUPSHELL.STARTING` once the API child is actually running.
- Keeps the Linux voice pill disabled by default and gates it behind `ELIZA_DESKTOP_PILL`, avoiding the duplicate loading-window surface on startup.
- Moves the pill shell to a minimal early mount path when explicitly enabled.
- Routes renderer imports through browser-safe local source entries for `@elizaos/ui` / `@elizaos/app-core`, reducing stale npm/dist and server-only barrel leakage.
- Syncs cloud public assets before desktop renderer builds and serves cloud media with correct MIME types.
- Defers the local-inference `TEXT_EMBEDDING` handler until a real Eliza-1 backend is active, so startup no longer aborts on embedding warmup before model activation.
- Adds missing package export subpaths and route-plugin specifiers needed for source-mode app/plugin loading.
- Adds tests for boot config bridging, crypto readiness, pill gating, renderer static assets, startup restore/runtime behavior, core plugin ordering, native module stubs, and i18n keys.

## Root Cause

The Linux desktop app had multiple startup races layered together:

1. In external dev/API mode, Electrobun could report a stale local `not_started` boot state even while `/api/health` already reported `agentState: running`, leaving the renderer stuck on the startup shell.
2. The desktop pill path created a second renderer surface by default and previously loaded the full app bundle before reaching the pill-only branch.
3. The renderer could resolve package roots through broad barrels or stale dist/npm package paths instead of local browser-safe source entries.
4. Local inference statically registered `TEXT_EMBEDDING` before a local backend was active, so startup probes could claim an embedding provider that could not serve yet.

- Adds signed app/runtime update manifest and model catalog schemas.

- Adds security smoke coverage and production-readiness docs for update signing, persistence, broker boundaries, SBOM/provenance, release gaps, and app/runtime packaging debt.
- Merges current `origin/develop` into this PR branch and resolves the workflow conflict with the homepage scope gate and upstream snapshot-baseline gate both preserved.

## Notes

This does not claim final enterprise production release completion. Remaining release infrastructure includes production key custody/revocation, hosted signed update channels, production SBOM/provenance publication, hardened runtime materialization, external sudoers/AppArmor/polkit/systemd/update-path security audit, Privacy Mode proof for embedded web/OAuth surfaces, and hardware/real-USB persistence validation.

Persistence creation cannot be fully proven from QEMU `-cdrom` boot because the ISO appears as read-only media; that must be validated from a real writable USB boot.

Runtime packaging is documented and validated as a build-time live-build overlay path, not a post-boot injection path. The current large Electrobun app tree remains acceptable for demo/RC validation, but the production target is a deterministic signed app artifact installed as the immutable factory runtime and updated only through the signed update-manager path.

36:T1508,## Summary

This draft keeps the Linux local-source desktop path current with `origin/develop` and fixes the pieces that were causing the app to wedge on the startup shell, spawn the pill unexpectedly, or fail to deliver local chat cleanly on the Lunar Lake laptop.

Local work is now merged with latest `origin/develop` as of `1b01940387` using merge commits, not rebase.

Notable fixes in this PR:

- `POST /api/agent/start` reports `agentState: "running"` instead of incorrectly setting `paused`; `/api/agent/pause` remains the explicit paused state.
- Electrobun `bootProgress` trusts the API child `/api/health.agentState` when wrapper status is stale, fixing the renderer staying on startup despite the backend being ready.
- The desktop pill window is gated behind the existing pill config instead of spawning unconditionally on Linux.
- Native plugin and app build scripts build/sync the required local workspace packages and shared-brand public assets so local-source builds do not fall through to stale npm/Bun-store artifacts.
- Package/release CI scripts follow the current `packages/scripts/*` layout, the moved `packages/cloud-sdk` workspace, and private workspace packages used only for manifest rewriting.
- Android mobile bundling avoids module-scope TLA in `packages/agent/src/bin.ts` and resolves viem's CJS transitive deps from the matching Bun store package versions instead of incidental top-level symlinks.
- iOS simulator smoke explicitly uses the compatibility runtime instead of requiring a full-Bun xcframework artifact.
- The app Vite config now resolves `@elizaos/app-core/api/ios-local-agent-transport` to local source, so clean CI/mobile checkouts do not require prebuilt `packages/app-core/dist` for that app-core subpath.

## Root Cause

The Linux desktop startup symptoms came from several integration mismatches at once:

- lifecycle start reported `paused`, while runtime health was actually running;
- renderer boot progress read stale wrapper state instead of authoritative API child health;
- the pill window spawned as a second Linux surface on every startup;
- local-source builds could resolve against stale package artifacts when workspace runtime packages/assets were not prepared first;
- after upstream moved scripts/packages, release and tarball CI still referenced old paths;
- Bun mobile bundling exposed viem CJS entrypoints without viem's own dependency graph, and Android module-scope `await` triggered the async `__esm` guard;
- iOS mobile Vite bundling imported an app-core subpath before `packages/app-core/dist` existed in a clean Actions checkout.

37:Tcfd,## Summary

Adds an Android native local-inference TTS playback path to TalkMode.

When the UI asks TalkMode to speak with `useLocalInferenceTts: true`, the Android plugin now:

- posts to the embedded agent at `127.0.0.1:31337/api/tts/local-inference`
- reads the per-boot local agent token from `files/auth/local-agent-token`
- accepts WAV/PCM audio from the local inference route
- parses PCM16 WAV headers on-device
- plays the PCM directly through `AudioTrack`
- emits a `playbackStart` event when real native playback begins
- supports `stopSpeaking()` cancellation by disconnecting the active HTTP request and stopping the active `AudioTrack`

The UI bridge now exposes the native TalkMode `speak`, `stopSpeaking`, `isSpeaking`, and `playbackStart` APIs, and `useVoiceChat` uses the native path on Capacitor platforms instead of routing local inference TTS through WebView `decodeAudioData`.

## Why

The stock Android APK local voice path needs to play local `/api/tts/local-inference` output from the native layer. Keeping playback in `AudioTrack` avoids WebView audio decode/playback as the integration point and makes this path ready for future chunked PCM/WAV streaming from the backend.

This PR does not claim the backend TTS synthesis is realtime yet. Pixel evidence shows native playback works, but the current Kokoro-backed endpoint is still buffered and synthesis is the bottleneck.

38:Tefb,## Summary

This fixes the stock Android APK local-inference activation path when `ELIZA_LOCAL_LLAMA=1` is enabled. `/api/local-inference/active` now activates the in-process Android native loader (`@elizaos/plugin-aosp-local-inference`) instead of trying the Capacitor bridge first, and `packages/agent` passes the live runtime into the local-inference route handler so non-Capacitor local activation can use the active runtime safely.

The AOSP local-inference bootstrap now exposes narrow activate/clear helpers around the already-registered `localInferenceLoader`, and the package barrel keeps those exports alive for the mobile bundle's tree-shaking rules.

## Why

The Pixel stock APK packages Shaw's fork llama.cpp libs and sets `ELIZA_LOCAL_LLAMA=1`. In that mode, the app should use the in-process bun:ffi loader, not the stock Capacitor llama bridge. The old route flow could still route model activation through the Capacitor bridge, which is the wrong loader for Eliza-1 Qwen35/QJL/Polar/TBQ GGUFs.
